from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: shape-dtype-only
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Shape/rank/dtype annotations only.
from functools import reduce

@zk_circuit
def extrema_op(a: Tuple[Integer, Float, Integer, Float], b: Tuple[Integer, Float, Integer, Float]):
    a_min_idx, a_min_val, a_max_idx, a_max_val = a
    b_min_idx, b_min_val, b_max_idx, b_max_val = b
    if a_min_val < b_min_val:
        if a_max_val > b_max_val:
            return a
        else:
            return (a_min_idx, a_min_val, b_max_idx, b_max_val)
    elif a_max_val > b_max_val:
        return (b_min_idx, b_min_val, a_max_idx, a_max_val)
    else:
        return b

@zk_chip
def extrema_id(x: Float):
    return (-1, 1.0, 1, 0.0)

@zk_chip
def indices(A: Float[Array, 'N<=1024']):
    return range(len(A))

@zk_chip
def extrema(x: Float[Array, 'N<=1024'], x_id: Tuple[Integer, Float, Integer, Float]):
    return reduce(extrema_op, zip(indices(x), x, indices(x), x), x_id)

@zk_circuit
@requires(lambda n, a: 0 <= n <= 1024)
def run_extrema(n: Integer, a: Float[Array, 'N<=1024']):
    a_id = extrema_id(0.0)
    return extrema(a, a_id)

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: data-dependent-loop, denominator-obligation, in-place-mutation
# Expected outcome: diagnostic/fail-closed candidate
# Precondition: Add an explicit iteration cap or classify as fail-closed until a bound is available.
# Precondition: Add nonzero or positive denominator facts at exact division/modulo sites when expressible.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
# Diagnostic: while loops require a public/padded maximum iteration count.

@zk_circuit
@requires(lambda n: 0 <= n <= 1024)
def factorize_naive(n: Integer):
    """ A naive factorization method. Take integer 'n', return list of
        factors.
    """
    if n < 2:
        return []
    factors = []
    p = 2
    while True:
        if n == 1:
            return factors
        r = n % p
        if r == 0:
            factors.append(p)
            n = n / p
        elif p * p >= n:
            factors.append(n)
            return factors
        elif p > 2:
            p += 2
        else:
            p += 1
    assert False, 'unreachable'

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: data-dependent-loop, in-place-mutation
# Expected outcome: diagnostic/fail-closed candidate
# Precondition: Add an explicit iteration cap or classify as fail-closed until a bound is available.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
# Diagnostic: while loops require a public/padded maximum iteration count.

@zk_chip
def fannkuch(n: Integer):
    count = list(range(1, n + 1))
    max_flips = 0
    m = n - 1
    r = n
    check = 0
    perm1 = list(range(n))
    perm = list(range(n))
    while 1:
        if check < 30:
            check += 1
        while r != 1:
            count[r - 1] = r
            r -= 1
        if perm1[0] != 0 and perm1[m] != m:
            perm = perm1[:]
            flips_count = 0
            k = perm[0]
            while k:
                perm[:k + 1] = perm[k::-1]
                flips_count += 1
                k = perm[0]
            if flips_count > max_flips:
                max_flips = flips_count
        while r != n:
            perm1.insert(r, perm1.pop(0))
            count[r] -= 1
            if count[r] > 0:
                break
            r += 1
        else:
            return max_flips

@zk_circuit
@requires(lambda n: 0 <= n <= 1024)
def fannkuch2(n: Integer):
    fannkuch(n)

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: in-place-mutation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.

@zk_circuit
def fbcorr(imgs: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024 K<=1024 L<=256'], filters: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024 K<=1024 L<=256'], output: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024 K<=1024 L<=256']):
    n_imgs, n_rows, n_cols, n_channels = imgs.shape
    n_filters, height, width, n_ch2 = filters.shape
    'omp parallel for'
    for ii in range(n_imgs):
        for rr in range(n_rows - height + 1):
            for cc in range(n_cols - width + 1):
                for hh in range(height):
                    for ww in range(width):
                        for jj in range(n_channels):
                            for ff in range(n_filters):
                                imgval = imgs[ii, rr + hh, cc + ww, jj]
                                filterval = filters[ff, hh, ww, jj]
                                output[ii, ff, rr, cc] += imgval * filterval

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: in-place-mutation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
import numpy as np

@zk_circuit
def fdtd(input_grid: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024'], steps: Integer):
    grid = input_grid.copy()
    old_grid = np.zeros_like(input_grid)
    previous_grid = np.zeros_like(input_grid)
    l_x = grid.shape[0]
    l_y = grid.shape[1]
    for i in range(steps):
        np.copyto(previous_grid, old_grid)
        np.copyto(old_grid, grid)
        for x in range(l_x):
            for y in range(l_y):
                grid[x, y] = 0.0
                if 0 < x + 1 < l_x:
                    grid[x, y] += old_grid[x + 1, y]
                if 0 < x - 1 < l_x:
                    grid[x, y] += old_grid[x - 1, y]
                if 0 < y + 1 < l_y:
                    grid[x, y] += old_grid[x, y + 1]
                if 0 < y - 1 < l_y:
                    grid[x, y] += old_grid[x, y - 1]
                grid[x, y] /= 2.0
                grid[x, y] -= previous_grid[x, y]
    return grid

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: complex, denominator-obligation
# Expected outcome: diagnostic/fail-closed candidate
# Precondition: Add nonzero or positive denominator facts at exact division/modulo sites when expressible.
# Diagnostic: Complex kernels require Complex dtype support or a fail-closed classification.
import math, numpy as np

@zk_circuit
def fft(x: Complex[Array, 'N<=1024']):
    N = x.shape[0]
    if N == 1:
        return np.array(x)
    e = fft(x[::2])
    o = fft(x[1::2])
    M = N // 2
    l = [e[k] + o[k] * math.e ** (-2j * math.pi * k / N) for k in range(M)]
    r = [e[k] - o[k] * math.e ** (-2j * math.pi * k / N) for k in range(M)]
    return np.array(l + r)

@zk_chip
def rfibo(n: Integer):
    if n < 2:
        return n
    else:
        n_1 = rfibo(n - 1)
        n_2 = rfibo(n - 2)
        return n_1 + n_2

@zk_chip
def fibo(n: Integer):
    if n < 10:
        return rfibo(n)
    else:
        n_1 = 0
        'omp task shared(n,n_1)'
        n_1 = fibo(n - 1)
        n_2 = fibo(n - 2)
        'omp taskwait'
        return n_1 + n_2

@zk_circuit

def test(n: Integer):
    """omp parallel"""
    'omp single'
    f = fibo(n)
    return f

gauss(n: Integer, a*: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024'], b*: Float[Array, 'N<=1024'])

Preconditions

  • Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.

Source excerpt 
from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: denominator-obligation, in-place-mutation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Add nonzero or positive denominator facts at exact division/modulo sites when expressible.
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.

@zk_chip
def pivot(n: Integer, i: Integer, a: Float[Array, 'N<=1024'], b: Float[Array, 'N<=1024']):
    i0 = i
    amp0 = abs(a[i - 1][i - 1])
    for j in range(i + 1, n + 1):
        amp = abs(a[i - 1][j - 1])
        if amp > amp0:
            i0 = j
            amp0 = amp
    if i == i0:
        return
    temp = b[i - 1]
    b[i - 1] = b[i0 - 1]
    b[i0 - 1] = temp
    for j in range(i, n + 1):
        temp = a[j - 1][i - 1]
        a[j - 1][i - 1] = a[j - 1][i0 - 1]
        a[j - 1][i0 - 1] = temp

@zk_circuit
@requires(lambda n, a, b: 0 <= n <= 1024)
def gauss(n: Integer, a: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024'], b: Float[Array, 'N<=1024']):
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        if i < n:
            pivot(n, i, a, b)
        a[i - 1][i - 1] = 1.0 / a[i - 1][i - 1]
        b[i - 1] = b[i - 1] * a[i - 1][i - 1]
        if i < n:
            for j in range(i + 1, n + 1):
                a[j - 1][i - 1] = a[j - 1][i - 1] * a[i - 1][i - 1]
            for k in range(i + 1, n + 1):
                b[k - 1] = b[k - 1] - a[i - 1][k - 1] * b[i - 1]
                for j in range(i + 1, n + 1):
                    a[j - 1][k - 1] = a[j - 1][k - 1] - a[i - 1][k - 1] * a[j - 1][i - 1]
    for i in range(n - 1, 0, -1):
        for j in range(i, n):
            b[i - 1] = b[i - 1] - a[j][i - 1] * b[j]
    return b

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: denominator-obligation
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Add nonzero or positive denominator facts at exact division/modulo sites when expressible.
import numpy

@zk_circuit
@requires(lambda inRGB, sigma: sigma != 0)
def goodExpoMeasure(inRGB: Float[Array, 'C<=3 H<=1024 W<=1024'], sigma: Float):
    """
    Compute the good exposition image quality measure on 1 input image.
    """
    R = inRGB[0, :, :].astype(numpy.float64)
    G = inRGB[1, :, :].astype(numpy.float64)
    B = inRGB[2, :, :].astype(numpy.float64)
    goodExpoR = numpy.exp(-(R - 128) ** 2 / sigma)
    goodExpoG = numpy.exp(-(G - 128) ** 2 / sigma)
    goodExpoB = numpy.exp(-(B - 128) ** 2 / sigma)
    goodExpo = goodExpoR * goodExpoG * goodExpoB
    goodExpo = (numpy.round(goodExpo, 2) * (2 ** 8 - 1)).astype(numpy.uint8)
    return goodExpo

@zk_circuit
def grouping(values: Integer[Array, 'N<=1024']):
    import numpy as np
    diff = np.concatenate(([1], np.diff(values)))
    idx = np.concatenate((np.where(diff)[0], [len(values)]))
    return (values[idx[:-1]], np.diff(idx))

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: denominator-obligation, domain-obligation, in-place-mutation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Add nonzero or positive denominator facts at exact division/modulo sites when expressible.
# Precondition: Add operation-specific domains: sqrt arg >= 0, log arg > 0, acos arg in [-1, 1].
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
import math
import numpy as np

@zk_chip
def window_floor(idx: Float, radius: Float):
    if radius > idx:
        return 0
    else:
        return idx - radius

@zk_chip
def window_ceil(idx: Float, ceil: Float, radius: Float):
    if idx + radius > ceil:
        return ceil
    else:
        return idx + radius

@zk_chip
def python_kernel(image: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024 K<=1024'], state: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024 K<=1024'], state_next: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024 K<=1024'], window_radius: Float):
    changes = 0
    sqrt_3 = math.sqrt(3.0)
    height = image.shape[0]
    width = image.shape[1]
    for j in range(width):
        for i in range(height):
            winning_colony = state[i, j, 0]
            defense_strength = state[i, j, 1]
            for jj in range(window_floor(j, window_radius), window_ceil(j + 1, width, window_radius)):
                for ii in range(window_floor(i, window_radius), window_ceil(i + 1, height, window_radius)):
                    if ii == i and jj == j:
                        continue
                    d = image[i, j, 0] - image[ii, jj, 0]
                    s = d * d
                    for k in range(1, 3):
                        d = image[i, j, k] - image[ii, jj, k]
                        s += d * d
                    gval = 1.0 - math.sqrt(s) / sqrt_3
                    attack_strength = gval * state[ii, jj, 1]
                    if attack_strength > defense_strength:
                        defense_strength = attack_strength
                        winning_colony = state[ii, jj, 0]
                        changes += 1
            state_next[i, j, 0] = winning_colony
            state_next[i, j, 1] = defense_strength
    return changes

@zk_circuit
@requires(lambda N: 0 <= N <= 1024)
def test(N: Integer):
    image = np.zeros((N, N, 3))
    state = np.zeros((N, N, 2))
    state_next = np.empty_like(state)
    state[0, 0, 0] = 1
    state[0, 0, 1] = 1
    return python_kernel(image, state, state_next, 10)

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: complex, data-dependent-loop, denominator-obligation, in-place-mutation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: diagnostic/fail-closed candidate
# Precondition: Add an explicit iteration cap or classify as fail-closed until a bound is available.
# Precondition: Add nonzero or positive denominator facts at exact division/modulo sites when expressible.
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
# Diagnostic: Complex kernels require Complex dtype support or a fail-closed classification.
# Diagnostic: while loops require a public/padded maximum iteration count.

@zk_circuit
@requires(lambda a, n, z, err, nter: 0 <= n <= 1024)
def guerre(a: Complex[Array, 'N<=1024'], n: Integer, z: Complex, err: Float, nter: Integer):
    az = [complex(0, 0) for i in range(50)]
    azz = [complex(0, 0) for i in range(50)]
    ci = complex(0.0, 1.0)
    eps = 1e-20
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        az[i - 1] = float(i) * a[i]
    for i in range(1, n):
        azz[i - 1] = float(i) * az[i]
    dz = err + 1
    itera = 0
    jter = 0
    while abs(dz) > err and itera < nter:
        p = a[n - 1] + a[n] * z
        for i in range(n - 1, 0, -1):
            p = a[i - 1] + z * p
        if abs(p) < eps:
            return z
        pz = az[n - 2] + az[n - 1] * z
        for i in range(n - 2, 0, -1):
            pz = az[i - 1] + z * pz
        pzz = azz[n - 3] + azz[n - 2] * z
        for i in range(n - 3, 0, -1):
            pzz = azz[i - 1] + z * pzz
        f = pz / p
        g = f ** 2 - pzz / p
        h = n * g
        amp1 = abs(f + h)
        amp2 = abs(f - h)
        if amp1 > amp2:
            dz = float(-n) / (f + h)
        else:
            dz = float(-n) / (f - h)
        itera = itera + 1
        jter = jter + 1
        if jter == 10:
            jter = 1
            dz = dz * ci
        z = z + dz
        if jter == 100:
            raise RuntimeError('Laguerre method not converging')
    return z

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: shape-dtype-only
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Shape/rank/dtype annotations only.
import numpy as np

@zk_circuit
def harris(I: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024']):
    m, n = I.shape
    dx = (I[1:, :] - I[:m - 1, :])[:, 1:]
    dy = (I[:, 1:] - I[:, :n - 1])[1:, :]
    A = dx * dx
    B = dy * dy
    C = dx * dy
    tr = A + B
    det = A * B - C * C
    k = 0.05
    return det - k * tr * tr

@zk_circuit
def fweb(y: Float[Array, 'N<=1024'], t: Float, a1: Float, a2: Float, b1: Float, b2: Float, d1: Float, d2: Float):
    yprime = np.empty((3,))
    yprime[0] = y[0] * (1.0 - y[0]) - a1 * y[0] * y[1] / (1.0 + b1 * y[0])

    yprime[2] = a2 * y[1] * y[2] / (1.0 + b2 * y[1]) - d2 * y[2]
    return yprime

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: denominator-obligation, in-place-mutation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Add nonzero or positive denominator facts at exact division/modulo sites when expressible.
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.

@zk_circuit
def histogram(data: Float[Array, 'N<=1024'], bin_width: Integer):
    lower_bound, upper_bound = (min(data), max(data))
    out_data = [0] * (1 + bin_width)
    for i in data:
        out_data[int(bin_width * (i - lower_bound) / (upper_bound - lower_bound))] += 1
    out_data[-2] += out_data[-1]
    out_data.pop()
    return out_data

import math

@zk_circuit
@requires(lambda xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax, step, range_, range_x, range_y, t: step != 0.0)
@requires(lambda xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax, step, range_, range_x, range_y, t: range_ != 0.0)
def run(xmin: Float, ymin: Float, xmax: Float, ymax: Float, step: Float, range_: Float, range_x: Integer, range_y: Integer, t: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024']):
    pt = [[0] * range_y for _ in range(range_x)]
    'omp parallel for'
    for i in range(range_x):

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: data-dependent-loop, in-place-mutation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: diagnostic/fail-closed candidate
# Precondition: Add an explicit iteration cap or classify as fail-closed until a bound is available.
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
# Diagnostic: while loops require a public/padded maximum iteration count.

@zk_circuit
def insertion_sort(list2: Float[Array, 'N<=1024']):
    for i in range(1, len(list2)):
        save = list2[i]
        j = i
        while j > 0 and list2[j - 1] > save:
            list2[j] = list2[j - 1]
            j -= 1
        list2[j] = save

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: in-place-mutation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
import numpy as np

@zk_circuit
def ising_step(field: Integer[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024'], beta: Float=0.4):
    N, M = field.shape
    for n_offset in range(2):
        for m_offset in range(2):
            for n in range(n_offset, N, 2):
                for m in range(m_offset, M, 2):
                    _ising_update(field, n, m, np.float32(beta))
    return field

@zk_chip
def clamp(v: Float, n: Integer):
    if v < 0:
        return v + n
    elif v < n:
        return v
    else:
        return v - n

@zk_chip
def _ising_update(field: Integer[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024'], n: Integer, m: Integer, beta: Float):
    total = 0
    N, M = field.shape
    for i in range(n - 1, n + 2):
        for j in range(m - 1, m + 2):
            if i == n and j == m:
                continue
            total += field[clamp(i, N), clamp(j, M)]
    dE = 2 * field[n, m] * total
    if dE <= 0:
        field[n, m] *= -1
    elif np.exp(-dE * beta) > 0.5:
        field[n, m] *= -1

Source excerpt 
from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: denominator-obligation, in-place-mutation
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Add nonzero or positive denominator facts at exact division/modulo sites when expressible.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
import numpy

@zk_circuit
@requires(lambda var, dh2: dh2 != 0.0)
def laplacian(var: Float[Array, 'N<=1024'], dh2: Float):
    """ (1D array, dx^2) -> laplacian(1D array)
    periodic_laplacian_1D_4th_order
    Implementing the 4th order 1D laplacian with periodic condition
    """
    lap = numpy.zeros_like(var)
    lap[1:] = 4.0 / 3.0 * var[:-1]
    lap[0] = 4.0 / 3.0 * var[1]
    lap[:-1] += 4.0 / 3.0 * var[1:]
    lap[-1] += 4.0 / 3.0 * var[0]
    lap += -5.0 / 2.0 * var
    lap[2:] += -1.0 / 12.0 * var[:-2]
    lap[:2] += -1.0 / 12.0 * var[-2:]
    lap[:-2] += -1.0 / 12.0 * var[2:]
    lap[-2:] += -1.0 / 12.0 * var[:2]
    return lap / dh2

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: denominator-obligation, in-place-mutation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Add nonzero or positive denominator facts at exact division/modulo sites when expressible.
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.

@zk_circuit
@requires(lambda In, Out, niter: 0 <= niter <= 1024)
def lapl2d(In: Float[Array, 'N<=1024'], Out: Float[Array, 'N<=1024'], niter: Integer):
    siz = In.shape[0]
    h2 = (1.0 / siz) ** 2
    for it in range(0, niter):
        Out[1:siz - 1, 1:siz - 1] = h2 * (In[0:siz - 2, 1:siz - 1] + In[1:siz - 1, 0:siz - 2] - 4.0 * In[1:siz - 1, 1:siz - 1] + In[2:siz, 1:siz - 1] + In[1:siz - 1, 2:siz])
        In, Out = (Out, In)

@zk_circuit
def laplacien(image: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024']):
    out_image = np.abs(4 * image[1:-1, 1:-1] - image[0:-2, 1:-1] - image[2:, 1:-1] - image[1:-1, 0:-2] - image[1:-1, 2:])
    valmax = np.max(out_image)
    valmax = max(1.0, valmax) + 1e-09
    out_image /= valmax
    return out_image

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: denominator-obligation, in-place-mutation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Add nonzero or positive denominator facts at exact division/modulo sites when expressible.
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.

@zk_chip
def update(u: Float[Array, 'N<=1024']):
    dx = 0.1
    dy = 0.1
    dx2 = dx * dx
    dy2 = dy * dy
    nx, ny = (len(u), len(u[0]))
    for i in range(1, nx - 1):
        for j in range(1, ny - 1):
            u[i][j] = ((u[i + 1][j] + u[i - 1][j]) * dy2 + (u[i][j + 1] + u[i][j - 1]) * dx2) / (2 * (dx2 + dy2))

@zk_circuit
@requires(lambda N, Niter: 0 <= N <= 1024)
@requires(lambda N, Niter: 0 <= Niter <= 1024)
def calc(N: Integer, Niter: Integer=100):
    u = [[0] * N for _ in range(N)]
    u[0] = [1] * N
    for i in range(Niter):
        update(u)
    return u

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: data-dependent-loop, denominator-obligation, domain-obligation, in-place-mutation, loop-bounded, matmul-dot
# Expected outcome: diagnostic/fail-closed candidate
# Precondition: Add an explicit iteration cap or classify as fail-closed until a bound is available.
# Precondition: Add nonzero or positive denominator facts at exact division/modulo sites when expressible.
# Precondition: Add operation-specific domains: sqrt arg >= 0, log arg > 0, acos arg in [-1, 1].
# Precondition: Declare shared matrix/vector dimensions explicitly; when not expressible as @requires, encode with matching symbolic annotation names and keep as review obligation.
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
# Diagnostic: while loops require a public/padded maximum iteration count.
import numpy as np
from math import log, tanh

@zk_chip
def phi0(x: Float):
    x = abs(x)
    if x < 9.08e-05:
        return 10
    else:
        return -log(tanh(x / 2))

@zk_chip
def G(Lq: Float[Array, 'N<=1024']):
    X = sum((phi0(e) for e in Lq))
    s = np.prod(np.sign(Lq))
    return s * phi0(X)

@zk_chip
def BinaryProduct(X: Integer[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024'], Y: Integer[Array, 'N<=1024']):
    """ Binary Matrices or Matrix-vector product in Z/2Z. Works with scipy.sparse.csr_matrix matrices X,Y too."""
    A = X.dot(Y)
    return A % 2

@zk_chip
def InCode(H: Integer[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024'], x: Integer[Array, 'N<=1024']):
    """ Computes Binary Product of H and x. If product is null, x is in the code.
        Returns appartenance boolean. 
    """
    return (BinaryProduct(H, x) == 0).all()

@zk_circuit
@requires(lambda H, Bits, Nodes, Lq, Lc, max_iter: 0 <= max_iter <= 1024)
def Decoding_logBP(H: Integer[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024'], Bits: Integer[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024'], Nodes: Integer[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024'], Lq: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024'], Lc: Float[Array, 'N<=1024'], max_iter: Integer=1):
    """ Decoding function using Belief Propagation algorithm (logarithmic version)
    IMPORTANT: if H is large (n>1000), H should be scipy.sparse.csr_matrix object to speed up calculations
    (highly recommanded. )
    -----------------------------------
    Parameters:
    
    H: 2D-array (OR scipy.sparse.csr_matrix object) Parity check matrix, shape = (m,n) 
    y: n-vector recieved after transmission in the channel. (In general, returned 
    by Coding Function)
    Signal-Noise Ratio: SNR = 10log(1/variance) in decibels of the AWGN used in coding.
    
    max_iter: (default = 1) max iterations of the main loop. Increase if decoding is not error-free.
     """
    m, n = H.shape
    if not len(Lc) == n:
        raise ValueError('La taille de y doit correspondre au nombre de colonnes de H')
    if m >= n:
        raise ValueError('H doit avoir plus de colonnes que de lignes')
    Lr = np.zeros(shape=(m, n))
    count = 0
    Lq += Lc
    while True:
        count += 1
        for i in range(m):
            Ni = Bits[i]
            for j in Ni:
                Nij = list(Ni)
                Nij.remove(j)
                Lr[i, j] = G(Lq[i][Nij])
        Lr = np.clip(Lr, -100, 100)
        for j in range(n):
            Mj = Nodes[j]
            for i in Mj:
                Mji = list(Mj)
                Mji.remove(i)

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: denominator-obligation, domain-obligation, in-place-mutation
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Add nonzero or positive denominator facts at exact division/modulo sites when expressible.
# Precondition: Add operation-specific domains: sqrt arg >= 0, log arg > 0, acos arg in [-1, 1].
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
import numpy

@zk_circuit
@requires(lambda data, mean, sigma: sigma != 0.0)
def log_likelihood(data: Float[Array, 'N<=1024'], mean: Float, sigma: Float):
    s = (data - mean) ** 2 / (2 * sigma ** 2)
    pdfs = numpy.exp(-s)
    pdfs /= numpy.sqrt(2 * numpy.pi) * sigma
    return numpy.log(pdfs).sum()

downsample(data: Float[Array, 'N<=1024'], n_out: Integer)

  • downsample: compile_error: In method "_areas_of_triangles", line 2 to 5
Source excerpt 
from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: in-place-mutation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
import numpy as np

@zk_chip
def _areas_of_triangles(a: Float[Array, 'N<=1024'], bs: Float, c: Float[Array, 'N<=1024']):
    bs_minus_a = bs - a
    a_minus_bs = a - bs
    return 0.5 * abs((a[0] - c[0]) * bs_minus_a[:, 1] - a_minus_bs[:, 0] * (c[1] - a[1]))

@zk_circuit
@requires(lambda data, n_out: 0 <= n_out <= 1024)
def downsample(data: Float[Array, 'N<=1024'], n_out: Integer):
    if n_out > data.shape[0]:
        raise ValueError('n_out must be <= number of rows in data')
    if n_out == data.shape[0]:
        return data
    if n_out < 3:
        raise ValueError('Can only downsample to a minimum of 3 points')
    n_bins = n_out - 2
    data_bins = np.array_split(data[1:len(data) - 1], n_bins)
    out = np.zeros((n_out, 2))
    out[0] = data[0]
    out[len(out) - 1] = data[len(data) - 1]
    for i in range(len(data_bins)):
        this_bin = data_bins[i]
        if i < n_bins - 1:
            next_bin = data_bins[i + 1]
        else:
            next_bin = data[len(data) - 1:]
        a = out[i]
        bs = this_bin
        c = next_bin.mean(axis=0)
        areas = _areas_of_triangles(a, bs, c)
        out[i + 1] = bs[np.argmax(areas)]
    return out

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: denominator-obligation, in-place-mutation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Add nonzero or positive denominator facts at exact division/modulo sites when expressible.
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
import numpy as np

@zk_circuit
def factorMatrix0(M: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024']):
    n = M.shape[0]
    m = M.shape[1]
    for line in range(0, n - 1):
        cmax = np.argmax(abs(M[line:n, line])) + line
        if cmax != line:
            M[[line, cmax]] = M[[cmax, line]]
        pivot = M[line, line]
        for j in range(line + 1, n):
            v = M[j, line] / pivot
            for k in range(line, m):
                M[j, k] -= v * M[line, k]

@zk_circuit
def factorMatrix1(M: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024']):
    n = M.shape[0]
    m = M.shape[1]
    for line in range(0, n - 1):
        cmax = line
        vmax = abs(M[line, line])
        for i in range(line + 1, n):
            if abs(M[i, line]) > vmax:
                vmax = abs(M[i, line])
                cmax = i
        if cmax != line:
            for j in range(line, m):
                t = M[line, j]
                M[line, j] = M[cmax, j]
                M[cmax, j] = t
        pivot = M[line, line]
        for j in range(line + 1, n):
            v = M[j, line] / pivot
            for k in range(line, m):
                M[j, k] -= v * M[line, k]

make_circle_two_points(points: Float, p: Float, q: Float)

Preconditions

  • Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.

Diagnostics / Blockers

0 ok / 1 errors / 0 timeouts across 1 entry circuit(s)

Source excerpt 
from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: denominator-obligation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Add nonzero or positive denominator facts at exact division/modulo sites when expressible.
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
import math

@zk_chip
def _cross_product(x0: Float, y0: Float, x1: Float, y1: Float, x2: Float, y2: Float):
    return (x1 - x0) * (y2 - y0) - (y1 - y0) * (x2 - x0)

@zk_chip
def make_circumcircle(a: Float[Array, 'N<=1024'], b: Float[Array, 'N<=1024'], c: Float[Array, 'N<=1024']):
    ox = (min(a[0], b[0], c[0]) + max(a[0], b[0], c[0])) / 2.0
    oy = (min(a[1], b[1], c[1]) + max(a[1], b[1], c[1])) / 2.0
    ax = a[0] - ox
    ay = a[1] - oy
    bx = b[0] - ox
    by = b[1] - oy
    cx = c[0] - ox
    cy = c[1] - oy
    d = (ax * (by - cy) + bx * (cy - ay) + cx * (ay - by)) * 2.0
    if d == 0.0:
        return None
    x = ox + ((ax * ax + ay * ay) * (by - cy) + (bx * bx + by * by) * (cy - ay) + (cx * cx + cy * cy) * (ay - by)) / d
    y = oy + ((ax * ax + ay * ay) * (cx - bx) + (bx * bx + by * by) * (ax - cx) + (cx * cx + cy * cy) * (bx - ax)) / d
    ra = math.hypot(x - a[0], y - a[1])
    rb = math.hypot(x - b[0], y - b[1])
    rc = math.hypot(x - c[0], y - c[1])
    return (x, y, max(ra, rb, rc))

@zk_chip
def make_diameter(a: Float[Array, 'N<=1024'], b: Float[Array, 'N<=1024']):
    cx = (a[0] + b[0]) / 2.0
    cy = (a[1] + b[1]) / 2.0
    r0 = math.hypot(cx - a[0], cy - a[1])
    r1 = math.hypot(cx - b[0], cy - b[1])
    return (cx, cy, max(r0, r1))

@zk_chip
def is_in_circle(c: Float[Array, 'N<=1024'], p: Float[Array, 'N<=1024']):
    return math.hypot(p[0] - c[0], p[1] - c[1]) <= c[2] * (1 + 1e-14)

@zk_circuit
def make_circle_two_points(points: Float, p: Float, q: Float):
    circ = make_diameter(p, q)
    left = None
    right = None
    px, py = p
    qx, qy = q
    for r in points:
        if is_in_circle(circ, r):
            continue
        cross = _cross_product(px, py, qx, qy, r[0], r[1])
        c = make_circumcircle(p, q, r)
        if c is None:
            continue
        elif cross > 0.0 and (left is None or _cross_product(px, py, qx, qy, c[0], c[1]) > _cross_product(px, py, qx, qy, left[0], left[1])):
            left = c
        elif cross < 0.0 and (right is None or _cross_product(px, py, qx, qy, c[0], c[1]) < _cross_product(px, py, qx, qy, right[0], right[1])):
            right = c
    if left is None and right is None:
        return circ
    elif left is None:
        return right
    elif right is None:
        return left
    else:
        return left if left[2] <= right[2] else right

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: complex, in-place-mutation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: diagnostic/fail-closed candidate
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
# Diagnostic: Complex kernels require Complex dtype support or a fail-closed classification.
import numpy as np

@zk_circuit
def md(E: Complex[Array, 'N<=1024'], symbols: Complex[Array, 'N<=1024']):
    L = E.shape[0]
    M = symbols.shape[0]
    syms_out = np.zeros(L, dtype=E.dtype)
    for i in range(L):
        im = np.argmin(abs(E[i] - symbols) ** 2)
        syms_out[i] = symbols[im]
    return syms_out

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: data-dependent-loop, denominator-obligation, in-place-mutation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: diagnostic/fail-closed candidate
# Precondition: Add an explicit iteration cap or classify as fail-closed until a bound is available.
# Precondition: Add nonzero or positive denominator facts at exact division/modulo sites when expressible.
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
# Diagnostic: while loops require a public/padded maximum iteration count.

@zk_circuit
@requires(lambda size, x_center, y_center, max_iteration: 0 <= size <= 1024)
@requires(lambda size, x_center, y_center, max_iteration: size != 0)
def mandel(size: Integer, x_center: Float, y_center: Float, max_iteration: Integer):
    out = [[0 for i in range(size)] for j in range(size)]
    for i in range(size):
        'omp parallel for'
        for j in range(size):
            x, y = (x_center + 4.0 * float(i - size / 2) / size, y_center + 4.0 * float(j - size / 2) / size)
            a, b = (0.0, 0.0)
            iteration = 0
            while a ** 2 + b ** 2 <= 4.0 and iteration < max_iteration:
                a, b = (a ** 2 - b ** 2 + x, 2 * a * b + y)
                iteration += 1
            if iteration == max_iteration:
                color_value = 255
            else:
                color_value = iteration * 10 % 255
            out[i][j] = color_value
    return out

matrix_multiply(m0: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024'], m1: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024'])

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: in-place-mutation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.

@zk_chip
def zero(n: Integer, m: Integer):
    return [[0 for row in range(n)] for col in range(m)]

@zk_circuit
def matrix_multiply(m0: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024'], m1: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024']):
    new_matrix = zero(len(m0), len(m1[0]))
    for i in range(len(m0)):
        for j in range(len(m1[0])):
            r = 0
            'omp parallel for reduction(+:r)'
            for k in range(len(m1)):
                r += m0[i][k] * m1[k][j]
            new_matrix[i][j] = r
    return new_matrix

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: in-place-mutation, linalg, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: diagnostic/fail-closed candidate
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Record square/invertible/SPD requirements where the linalg call needs them.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
# Diagnostic: np.linalg kernels may stay fail-closed until support is selected.
import numpy as np

@zk_circuit
def matrix_class_distance(dat: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024'], dat_filter: Integer[Array, 'N<=1024'], dat_points: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024'], iterations: Integer):
    aggregation = 0
    for i in range(iterations):
        aggregation += np.sum(np.linalg.norm(dat[dat_filter == i] - dat_points[i], axis=1))
    return aggregation

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: denominator-obligation, in-place-mutation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Add nonzero or positive denominator facts at exact division/modulo sites when expressible.
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
import math

@zk_circuit
@requires(lambda xmin, xmax, numSteps, rand, randsize: numSteps != 0)
@requires(lambda xmin, xmax, numSteps, rand, randsize: randsize != 0)
def montecarlo_integration(xmin: Float, xmax: Float, numSteps: Integer, rand: Float[Array, 'N<=1024'], randsize: Integer):

    @zk_chip
    def f(x: Float):
        return math.sin(x)
    ymin = f(xmin)
    ymax = ymin
    for i in range(numSteps):
        x = xmin + (xmax - xmin) * float(i) / numSteps
        y = f(x)
        if y < ymin:
            ymin = y
        if y > ymax:
            ymax = y
    rectArea = (xmax - xmin) * (ymax - ymin)
    numPoints = numSteps
    ctr = 0
    for j in range(numPoints):
        x = xmin + (xmax - xmin) * rand[j % randsize]
        y = ymin + (ymax - ymin) * rand[j % randsize]
        if math.fabs(y) <= math.fabs(f(x)):
            if f(x) > 0 and y > 0 and (y <= f(x)):
                ctr += 1
            if f(x) < 0 and y < 0 and (y >= f(x)):
                ctr -= 1
    fnArea = rectArea * float(ctr) / numPoints
    return fnArea

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: denominator-obligation, in-place-mutation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Add nonzero or positive denominator facts at exact division/modulo sites when expressible.
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
from numpy import empty_like

@zk_circuit
@requires(lambda x, k: 0 <= k <= 1024)
def dilate_decompose_loops(x: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024'], k: Integer):
    m, n = x.shape
    y = empty_like(x)
    for i in range(m):
        for j in range(n):
            left_idx = max(0, i - k // 2)
            right_idx = min(m, i + k // 2 + 1)
            currmax = x[left_idx, j]
            for ii in range(left_idx + 1, right_idx):
                elt = x[ii, j]
                if elt > currmax:
                    currmax = elt
            y[i, j] = currmax
    z = empty_like(x)
    for i in range(m):
        for j in range(n):
            left_idx = max(0, j - k // 2)
            right_idx = min(n, j + k // 2 + 1)
            currmax = y[i, left_idx]
            for jj in range(left_idx + 1, right_idx):
                elt = y[i, jj]
                if elt > currmax:
                    currmax = elt
            z[i, j] = currmax
    return z

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: data-dependent-loop, in-place-mutation
# Expected outcome: diagnostic/fail-closed candidate
# Precondition: Add an explicit iteration cap or classify as fail-closed until a bound is available.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
# Diagnostic: while loops require a public/padded maximum iteration count.

@zk_circuit
@requires(lambda x, y, m: 0 <= m <= 1024)
def gf2mulmod(x: Float, y: Float, m: Integer):
    z = 0
    while x > 0:
        if x & 1 != 0:
            z ^= y
        y <<= 1
        y2 = y ^ m
        if y2 < y:
            y = y2
        x >>= 1
    return z

@zk_circuit
def check2(x: Float, y: Float):

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: denominator-obligation, in-place-mutation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Add nonzero or positive denominator facts at exact division/modulo sites when expressible.
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
import numpy as np

@zk_circuit
@requires(lambda pos, offset, bound: bound != 0.0)
def wrap(pos: Float, offset: Float, bound: Float):
    return (pos + offset) % bound

@zk_circuit
def clamp(pos: Float, offset: Float, bound: Float):
    return min(bound - 1, max(0, pos + offset))

@zk_circuit
def reflect(pos: Float, offset: Float, bound: Float):
    idx = pos + offset
    return min(2 * (bound - 1) - idx, max(idx, -idx))

@zk_circuit
def local_maxima(data: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024 K<=1024 L<=256'], mode: Float=wrap):
    wsize = data.shape
    result = np.ones(data.shape, bool)
    for pos in np.ndindex(data.shape):
        myval = data[pos]
        for offset in np.ndindex(wsize):
            neighbor_idx = tuple((mode(p, o - w // 2, w) for p, o, w in zip(pos, offset, wsize)))
            result[pos] &= data[neighbor_idx] <= myval
    return result

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: data-dependent-loop, in-place-mutation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: diagnostic/fail-closed candidate
# Precondition: Add an explicit iteration cap or classify as fail-closed until a bound is available.
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
# Diagnostic: while loops require a public/padded maximum iteration count.

@zk_chip
def permutations(iterable: Float, r: Integer=None):
    """permutations(range(3), 2) --> (0,1) (0,2) (1,0) (1,2) (2,0) (2,1)"""
    pool = tuple(iterable)
    n = len(pool)
    if r is None:
        r = n
    indices = list(range(n))
    cycles = list(range(n - r + 1, n + 1))[::-1]
    yield tuple((pool[i] for i in indices[:r]))
    while n:
        for i in reversed(range(r)):
            cycles[i] -= 1
            if cycles[i] == 0:
                indices[i:] = indices[i + 1:] + indices[i:i + 1]
                cycles[i] = n - i
            else:
                j = cycles[i]
                indices[i], indices[-j] = (indices[-j], indices[i])
                yield tuple((pool[i] for i in indices[:r]))
                break
        else:
            return

@zk_circuit
def n_queens(queen_count: Integer):
    """N-Queens solver.

    Args:
        queen_count: the number of queens to solve for. This is also the
            board size.

    Yields:
        Solutions to the problem. Each yielded value is looks like
        (3, 8, 2, 1, 4, ..., 6) where each number is the column position for the
        queen, and the index into the tuple indicates the row.
    """
    out = list()
    cols = list(range(queen_count))
    for vec in permutations(cols, None):
        if queen_count == len(set((vec[i] + i for i in cols))) == len(set((vec[i] - i for i in cols))):
            out.append(vec)
    return out

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: domain-obligation, in-place-mutation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Add operation-specific domains: sqrt arg >= 0, log arg > 0, acos arg in [-1, 1].
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
import numpy as np

@zk_circuit
def pairwise(X: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024']):
    M, N = X.shape
    D = np.empty((M, M))
    for i in range(M):
        for j in range(M):
            d = 0.0
            for k in range(N):
                tmp = X[i, k] - X[j, k]
                d += tmp * tmp
            D[i, j] = np.sqrt(d)
    return D

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: denominator-obligation, domain-obligation, in-place-mutation
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Add nonzero or positive denominator facts at exact division/modulo sites when expressible.
# Precondition: Add operation-specific domains: sqrt arg >= 0, log arg > 0, acos arg in [-1, 1].
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
import numpy as np

@zk_circuit
def dist(x: Float[Array, 'N<=1024'], y: Float[Array, 'N<=1024'], z: Float[Array, 'N<=1024'], L: Integer, periodicX: Bool, periodicY: Bool, periodicZ: Bool):
    """ Computes distances between all particles and places the result in a matrix such that the ij th matrix entry corresponds to the distance between particle i and j """
    N = len(x)
    xtemp = np.tile(x, (N, 1))
    dx = xtemp - xtemp.T
    ytemp = np.tile(y, (N, 1))
    dy = ytemp - ytemp.T
    ztemp = np.tile(z, (N, 1))
    dz = ztemp - ztemp.T
    if periodicX:
        dx[dx > L / 2] = dx[dx > L / 2] - L
        dx[dx < -L / 2] = dx[dx < -L / 2] + L
    if periodicY:
        dy[dy > L / 2] = dy[dy > L / 2] - L
        dy[dy < -L / 2] = dy[dy < -L / 2] + L
    if periodicZ:
        dz[dz > L / 2] = dz[dz > L / 2] - L
        dz[dz < -L / 2] = dz[dz < -L / 2] + L
    d = np.sqrt(dx ** 2 + dy ** 2 + dz ** 2)
    d[d == 0] = -1
    return (d, dx, dy, dz)

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: data-dependent-loop, in-place-mutation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: diagnostic/fail-closed candidate
# Precondition: Add an explicit iteration cap or classify as fail-closed until a bound is available.
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
# Diagnostic: while loops require a public/padded maximum iteration count.

@zk_circuit
def permutations(iterable: Integer[Array, 'N<=1024']):
    """permutations(range(3), 2) --> (0,1) (0,2) (1,0) (1,2) (2,0) (2,1)"""
    out = []
    pool = tuple(iterable)
    n = len(pool)
    r = n
    indices = list(range(n))
    cycles = list(range(n - r + 1, n + 1))[::-1]
    out.append(tuple([pool[i] for i in indices[:r]]))
    while 1:
        for i in reversed(range(r)):
            cycles[i] -= 1
            if cycles[i] == 0:
                indices[i:] = indices[i + 1:] + indices[i:i + 1]
                cycles[i] = n - i
            else:
                j = cycles[i]
                indices[i], indices[-j] = (indices[-j], indices[i])
                out.append(tuple([pool[i] for i in indices[:r]]))
                break
        else:
            return out

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: complex, in-place-mutation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: diagnostic/fail-closed candidate
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
# Diagnostic: Complex kernels require Complex dtype support or a fail-closed classification.

@zk_circuit
@requires(lambda n, i, a, b: 0 <= n <= 1024)
def pivot(n: Integer, i: Integer, a: Complex[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024'], b: Complex[Array, 'N<=1024']):
    i0 = i
    amp0 = abs(a[i - 1][i - 1])
    for j in range(i + 1, n + 1):
        amp = abs(a[i - 1][j - 1])
        if amp > amp0:
            i0 = j
            amp0 = amp
    if i == i0:
        return
    temp = b[i - 1]
    b[i - 1] = b[i0 - 1]
    b[i0 - 1] = temp
    for j in range(i, n + 1):
        temp = a[j - 1][i - 1]
        a[j - 1][i - 1] = a[j - 1][i0 - 1]
        a[j - 1][i0 - 1] = temp
    return (a, b)

primes_sieve(limit: Integer)

0 ok / 1 errors / 0 timeouts across 1 entry circuit(s)

Source excerpt 
from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: in-place-mutation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.

@zk_circuit
def primes_sieve(limit: Integer):
    a = [True] * limit
    a[0] = a[1] = False
    primes = list()
    for i, isprime in enumerate(a):
        if isprime:
            primes.append(i)
            for n in range(i * i, limit, i):
                a[n] = False
    return primes

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.

# Expected outcome: diagnostic/fail-closed candidate

# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
# Diagnostic: while loops require a public/padded maximum iteration count.

@zk_circuit
@requires(lambda n: 0 <= n <= 1024)
def get_primes7(n: Integer):
    """
	standard optimized sieve algorithm to get a list of prime numbers
	--- this is the function to compare your functions against! ---
	"""
    if n < 2:
        return []
    if n == 2:
        return [2]
    s = list(range(3, n + 1, 2))
    mroot = n ** 0.5
    half = len(s)
    i = 0
    m = 3
    while m <= mroot:
        if s[i]:
            j = (m * m - 3) // 2
            s[j] = 0
            while j < half:
                s[j] = 0
                j += m
        i = i + 1
        m = 2 * i + 3
    return [2] + [x for x in s if x]

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: denominator-obligation, sort-permutation
# Expected outcome: diagnostic/fail-closed candidate
# Precondition: Add nonzero or positive denominator facts at exact division/modulo sites when expressible.
# Diagnostic: Sort/permutation kernels should remain explicit diagnostic rows until supported.
import numpy as np

@zk_circuit
def projection_simplex(v: Float[Array, 'N<=1024'], z: Integer=1):
    """
    Old implementation for test and benchmark purposes.
    The arguments v and z should be a vector and a scalar, respectively.
    """
    n_features = v.shape[0]
    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv = np.cumsum(u) - z
    ind = np.arange(n_features) + 1
    cond = u - cssv / ind > 0
    rho = ind[cond][-1]
    theta = cssv[cond][-1] / float(rho)
    w = np.maximum(v - theta, 0)
    return w

Source excerpt 
from zinnia import *

@zk_chip
def hits(x1: Integer, y1: Integer, x2: Integer, y2: Integer):
    """Check whether a queen positioned at (x1, y1) will hit a queen at position (x2, y2)"""
    return x1 == x2 or y1 == y2 or abs(x1 - x2) == abs(y1 - y2)

@zk_chip
def hitsany(x: Integer, y: Integer, queens_x: List[Integer], queens_y: List[Integer]):
    """Check whether a queen positioned at (x1, y1) will hit any other queen"""
    for i in range(len(queens_x)):
        if hits(x, y, queens_x[i], queens_y[i]):
            return True
    return False

@zk_chip
def _solve(n: Integer, queens_x: List[Integer], queens_y: List[Integer]):
    """Solve the queens puzzle"""
    if n == 0:
        return True
    for x in range(1, 9):
        for y in range(1, 9):
            if not hitsany(x, y, queens_x, queens_y):
                queens_x.append(x)
                queens_y.append(y)
                if _solve(n - 1, queens_x, queens_y):
                    return True
                queens_x.pop()
                queens_y.pop()
    return False

@zk_circuit
@requires(lambda n: 0 <= n <= 1024)
def solve(n: Integer):
    queens_x = []
    queens_y = []
    if _solve(n, queens_x, queens_y):
        return (queens_x, queens_y)
    else:
        return None

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: data-dependent-loop, in-place-mutation
# Expected outcome: diagnostic/fail-closed candidate
# Precondition: Add an explicit iteration cap or classify as fail-closed until a bound is available.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
# Diagnostic: while loops require a public/padded maximum iteration count.

@zk_chip
def partition(list: Float[Array, 'N<=1024'], start: Float, end: Float):
    pivot = list[end]
    bottom = start - 1
    top = end
    done = 0
    while not done:
        while not done:
            bottom = bottom + 1
            if bottom == top:
                done = 1
                break
            if list[bottom] > pivot:
                list[top] = list[bottom]
                break
        while not done:
            top = top - 1
            if top == bottom:
                done = 1
                break
            if list[top] < pivot:
                list[bottom] = list[top]
                break
    list[top] = pivot
    return top

@zk_chip
def do_quicksort(list: Float, start: Float, end: Float):
    if start < end:
        split = partition(list, start, end)
        do_quicksort(list, start, split - 1)
        do_quicksort(list, split + 1, end)

@zk_circuit
def quicksort(l: Integer[Array, 'N<=1024'], s: Integer, e: Integer):
    do_quicksort(l, s, e)

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: complex, data-dependent-loop, denominator-obligation, domain-obligation, in-place-mutation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: diagnostic/fail-closed candidate
# Precondition: Add an explicit iteration cap or classify as fail-closed until a bound is available.
# Precondition: Add nonzero or positive denominator facts at exact division/modulo sites when expressible.
# Precondition: Add operation-specific domains: sqrt arg >= 0, log arg > 0, acos arg in [-1, 1].
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
# Diagnostic: Complex kernels require Complex dtype support or a fail-closed classification.
# Diagnostic: while loops require a public/padded maximum iteration count.
import cmath

@zk_chip
def guerre(a: Complex[Array, 'N<=1024'], n: Integer, z: Float, err: Float, nter: Float):
    az = [complex(0, 0) for i in range(50)]
    azz = [complex(0, 0) for i in range(50)]
    ci = complex(0.0, 1.0)
    eps = 1e-20
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        az[i - 1] = float(i) * a[i]
    for i in range(1, n):
        azz[i - 1] = float(i) * az[i]
    dz = err + 1
    itera = 0
    jter = 0
    while abs(dz) > err and itera < nter:
        p = a[n - 1] + a[n] * z
        for i in range(n - 1, 0, -1):
            p = a[i - 1] + z * p
        if abs(p) < eps:
            return z
        pz = az[n - 2] + az[n - 1] * z
        for i in range(n - 2, 0, -1):
            pz = az[i - 1] + z * pz
        pzz = azz[n - 3] + azz[n - 2] * z
        for i in range(n - 3, 0, -1):
            pzz = azz[i - 1] + z * pzz
        f = pz / p
        g = f ** 2 - pzz / p
        h = n * g
        amp1 = abs(f + h)
        amp2 = abs(f - h)
        if amp1 > amp2:
            dz = float(-n) / (f + h)
        else:
            dz = float(-n) / (f - h)
        itera = itera + 1
        jter = jter + 1
        if jter == 10:
            jter = 1
            dz = dz * ci
        z = z + dz
        if jter == 100:
            raise RuntimeError('Laguerre method not converging')
    return z

@zk_chip
def fndrt(a: Complex[Array, 'N<=1024'], n: Integer):
    z = [complex(0, 0) for k in range(n)]
    if n == 1:
        z[0] = -a[0] / a[1]
        return z
    if n > 2:
        for k in range(n, 2, -1):
            root = complex(0.0, 0)
            err = 1e-12
            root = guerre(a, k, root, err, 1000)
            err = 0.0
            root = guerre(a, k, root, err, 5)
            z[k - 1] = root
            for i in range(k, 0, -1):
                a[i - 1] = a[i - 1] + root * a[i]
            for i in range(1, k + 1):
                a[i - 1] = a[i]
    z[1] = 0.5 * (-a[1] + cmath.sqrt(a[1] * a[1] - 4.0 * a[0] * a[2])) / a[2]
    z[0] = 0.5 * (-a[1] - cmath.sqrt(a[1] * a[1] - 4.0 * a[0] * a[2])) / a[2]
    return z

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: denominator-obligation, in-place-mutation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Add nonzero or positive denominator facts at exact division/modulo sites when expressible.
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
import numpy as np
from numpy import trace, concatenate, dot
from numpy.random import randn
from numpy.linalg import matrix_power

@zk_circuit
def rand_mat_stat(t: Float[Array, 'N<=1024']):
    n = 5
    v = np.empty(t)
    w = np.empty(t)
    for i in range(t):
        a = randn(n, n)
        b = randn(n, n)
        c = randn(n, n)
        d = randn(n, n)
        P = concatenate((a, b, c, d), axis=1)
        Q = concatenate((concatenate((a, b), axis=1), concatenate((c, d), axis=1)), axis=0)
        v[i] = trace(matrix_power(dot(P.T, P), 4))
        w[i] = trace(matrix_power(dot(Q.T, Q), 4))
    return (np.std(v) / np.mean(v), np.std(w) / np.mean(w))

from zinnia import *

@zk_circuit
def rc4_crypt(data: str, key: Float[Array, 'N<=1024']):
    S = range(256)
    j = 0
    out = []
    for i in range(256):
        j = (j + S[i] + ord(key[i % len(key)])) % 256
        S[i], S[j] = (S[j], S[i])
    for char in data:
        i = j = 0
        i = (i + 1) % 256
        j = (j + S[i]) % 256
        S[i], S[j] = (S[j], S[i])
        out.append(chr(ord(char) ^ S[(S[i] + S[j]) % 256]))
    return ''.join(out)

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: in-place-mutation
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
import numpy as np

@zk_circuit
def repeating(x: Float[Array, 'N<=1024'], nvar_y: Integer):
    nvar_x = x.shape[0]
    y = np.empty(nvar_x * (1 + nvar_y))
    y[0:nvar_x] = x[0:nvar_x]
    y[nvar_x:] = np.repeat(x, nvar_y)
    return y

from zinnia import *

@zk_circuit
def decode(roman: Float[Array, 'N<=1024']):
    s, t = ('MDCLXVI', (1000, 500, 100, 50, 10, 5, 1))
    _rdecode = dict(zip(s, t))
    result = 0
    for r, r1 in zip(roman, roman[1:]):
        rd, rd1 = (_rdecode[r], _rdecode[r1])
        result += -rd if rd < rd1 else rd
    return result + _rdecode[roman[-1]]

# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate

@zk_circuit
def scrabble_fun_score(word: str, scoretable: Float):
    return sum([scoretable.get(x, 0) for x in word])

@zk_circuit
def scrabble_imp_score(word: str, scoretable: Float[Array, 'N<=1024']):
    score = 0
    for letter in word:
        if letter in scoretable:
            score += scoretable[letter]
    return score

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: denominator-obligation
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Add nonzero or positive denominator facts at exact division/modulo sites when expressible.

@zk_chip
def is_prime(n: Integer):
    return all((n % j > 0 for j in range(2, n)))

@zk_circuit
def primes_below(x: Integer):
    return [[j - 6, j] for j in range(9, x + 1) if is_prime(j) and is_prime(j - 6)]

import numpy as np

@zk_circuit
def model(height: Float[Array, 'N<=1024'], width: Float[Array, 'N<=1024'], dtype: Float):
    m = np.ones((height, width), dtype=dtype)
    m[height // 4, width // 4] = 6.0
    return m

@zk_chip
def step(H: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024'], U: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024'], V: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024'], dt: Float=0.02, dx: Float=1.0, dy: Float=1.0):
    g = 9.80665
    H[:, 0] = H[:, 1]
    U[:, 0] = U[:, 1]
    V[:, 0] = -V[:, 1]
    H[:, -1] = H[:, -2]
    U[:, -1] = U[:, -2]
    V[:, -1] = -V[:, -2]
    H[0, :] = H[1, :]
    U[0, :] = -U[1, :]
    V[0, :] = V[1, :]
    H[-1, :] = H[-2, :]
    U[-1, :] = -U[-2, :]
    V[-1, :] = V[-2, :]
    Hx = (H[1:, 1:-1] + H[:-1, 1:-1]) // 2 - dt // (2 * dx) * (U[1:, 1:-1] - U[:-1, 1:-1])
    Ux = (U[1:, 1:-1] + U[:-1, 1:-1]) // 2 - dt / (2 * dx) * (U[1:, 1:-1] ** 2 // H[1:, 1:-1] + g // 2 * H[1:, 1:-1] ** 2 - (U[:-1, 1:-1] ** 2 // H[:-1, 1:-1] + g // 2 * H[:-1, 1:-1] ** 2))
    Vx = (V[1:, 1:-1] + V[:-1, 1:-1]) // 2 - dt // (2 * dx) * (U[1:, 1:-1] * V[1:, 1:-1] // H[1:, 1:-1] - U[:-1, 1:-1] * V[:-1, 1:-1] // H[:-1, 1:-1])
    Hy = (H[1:-1, 1:] + H[1:-1, :-1]) // 2 - dt // (2 * dy) * (V[1:-1, 1:] - V[1:-1, :-1])
    Uy = (U[1:-1, 1:] + U[1:-1, :-1]) // 2 - dt // (2 * dy) * (V[1:-1, 1:] * U[1:-1, 1:] // H[1:-1, 1:] - V[1:-1, :-1] * U[1:-1, :-1] // H[1:-1, :-1])
    Vy = (V[1:-1, 1:] + V[1:-1, :-1]) // 2 - dt // (2 * dy) * (V[1:-1, 1:] ** 2 // H[1:-1, 1:] + g // 2 * H[1:-1, 1:] ** 2 - (V[1:-1, :-1] ** 2 // H[1:-1, :-1] + g // 2 * H[1:-1, :-1] ** 2))
    H[1:-1, 1:-1] -= dt // dx * (Ux[1:, :] - Ux[:-1, :]) + dt // dy * (Vy[:, 1:] - Vy[:, :-1])
    U[1:-1, 1:-1] -= dt // dx * (Ux[1:, :] ** 2 // Hx[1:, :] + g // 2 * Hx[1:, :] ** 2 - (Ux[:-1, :] ** 2 // Hx[:-1, :] + g // 2 * Hx[:-1, :] ** 2)) + dt // dy * (Vy[:, 1:] * Uy[:, 1:] // Hy[:, 1:] - Vy[:, :-1] * Uy[:, :-1] // Hy[:, :-1])
    V[1:-1, 1:-1] -= dt // dx * (Ux[1:, :] * Vx[1:, :] // Hx[1:, :] - Ux[:-1, :] * Vx[:-1, :] // Hx[:-1, :]) + dt // dy * (Vy[:, 1:] ** 2 // Hy[:, 1:] + g // 2 * Hy[:, 1:] ** 2 - (Vy[:, :-1] ** 2 // Hy[:, :-1] + g // 2 * Hy[:, :-1] ** 2))
    return (H, U, V)

@zk_circuit
def simulate(H: Float[Array, 'N<=1024'], timesteps: Float):
    U = np.zeros_like(H)
    V = np.zeros_like(H)
    for i in range(timesteps):
        H, U, V = step(H, U, V)
    return H

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: in-place-mutation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
from numpy import zeros, power, tanh

@zk_circuit
def slowparts(d: Integer, re: Integer, preDz: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024 K<=1024'], preWz: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024 K<=1024'], SRW: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024'], RSW: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024'], yxV: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024 K<=1024'], xyU: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024 K<=1024'], resid: Integer):
    """ computes the linear algebra intensive part of the gradients of the grae
    """
    fprime = lambda x: 1 - power(tanh(x), 2)
    partialDU = zeros((d + 1, re, 2 * d, d))
    for k in range(2 * d):
        for i in range(d):
            partialDU[:, :, k, i] = fprime(preDz[k]) * fprime(preWz[i]) * (SRW[i, k] + RSW[i, k]) * yxV[:, :, i]
    return partialDU

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: in-place-mutation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.

@zk_circuit
def smoothing(x: Float[Array, 'N<=1024'], alpha: Float):
    """
  Exponential smoothing of a time series
  For x = 10**6 floats
  - Python runtime: 9 seconds
  - Parakeet runtime: .01 seconds
  """
    s = x.copy()
    for i in range(1, len(x)):
        s[i] = alpha * x[i] + (1 - alpha) * s[i - 1]
    return s

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: in-place-mutation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.

@zk_circuit
def sobelFilter(original_image: Integer[Array, 'N<=1024'], cols: Integer, rows: Integer):
    edge_image = list(range(len(original_image)))
    for i in range(rows):
        edge_image[i * cols] = 255
        edge_image[(i + 1) * cols - 1] = 255
    for i in range(1, cols - 1):
        edge_image[i] = 255
        edge_image[i + (rows - 1) * cols] = 255
    for iy in range(1, rows - 1):
        for ix in range(1, cols - 1):
            sum_x = 0
            sum_y = 0
            sum = 0
            sum_x += original_image[ix - 1 + (iy - 1) * cols] * -1
            sum_x += original_image[ix + (iy - 1) * cols] * -2
            sum_x += original_image[ix + 1 + (iy - 1) * cols] * -1
            sum_x += original_image[ix - 1 + (iy + 1) * cols] * 1
            sum_x += original_image[ix + (iy + 1) * cols] * 2
            sum_x += original_image[ix + 1 + (iy + 1) * cols] * 1
            sum_x = min(255, max(0, sum_x))
            sum_y += original_image[ix - 1 + (iy - 1) * cols] * 1
            sum_y += original_image[ix + 1 + (iy - 1) * cols] * -1
            sum_y += original_image[ix - 1 + iy * cols] * 2
            sum_y += original_image[ix + 1 + iy * cols] * -2
            sum_y += original_image[ix - 1 + (iy + 1) * cols] * 1
            sum_y += original_image[ix + 1 + (iy + 1) * cols] * -1
            sum_y = min(255, max(0, sum_y))
            sum = abs(sum_x) + abs(sum_y)
            edge_image[ix + iy * cols] = 255 - (255 & sum)
    return edge_image

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: complex, in-place-mutation
# Expected outcome: diagnostic/fail-closed candidate
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
# Diagnostic: Complex kernels require Complex dtype support or a fail-closed classification.

@zk_chip
def cross1(c: Complex[Array, 'N<=1024'], a: Complex[Array, 'N<=1024'], b: Complex[Array, 'N<=1024']):
    c[0] = a[0] * b[2] - a[2] * b[1]
    c[1] = a[2] * b[0] - a[0] * b[2]
    c[2] = a[0] * b[1] - a[1] * b[0]
    return c

@zk_circuit
def cross2(c: Integer, a: Float, b: Float):
    cross1(c, a, b)
    c *= 1j
    return c

@zk_circuit
@requires(lambda dU, U_hat, K2, K, P_hat, K_over_K2, nu: 0 <= K <= 1024)
def add_pressure_diffusion(dU: Complex[Array, 'N<=1024'], U_hat: Float, K2: Float, K: Integer, P_hat: Complex[Array, 'N<=1024'], K_over_K2: Complex[Array, 'N<=1024'], nu: Float):
    du0, du1, du2 = (dU[0], dU[1], dU[2])
    k_0, k_1, k_2 = (K_over_K2[0], K_over_K2[1], K_over_K2[2])
    P_hat[:] = du0 * k_0 + du1 * k_1 + du2 * k_2
    dU[:] = dU - P_hat * K - nu * K2 * U_hat
    return P_hat

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: data-dependent-loop, denominator-obligation, domain-obligation, in-place-mutation
# Expected outcome: diagnostic/fail-closed candidate
# Precondition: Add an explicit iteration cap or classify as fail-closed until a bound is available.
# Precondition: Add nonzero or positive denominator facts at exact division/modulo sites when expressible.
# Precondition: Add operation-specific domains: sqrt arg >= 0, log arg > 0, acos arg in [-1, 1].
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
# Diagnostic: while loops require a public/padded maximum iteration count.
import math

@zk_chip
def isprime(n: Integer):
    """Returns True if n is prime and False otherwise"""
    if n < 2:
        return False
    if n == 2:
        return True
    max = int(math.ceil(math.sqrt(n)))
    i = 2
    while i <= max:
        if n % i == 0:
            return False
        i += 1
    return True

@zk_circuit
@requires(lambda n: 0 <= n <= 1024)
def sum_primes(n: Integer):
    """Calculates sum of all primes below given integer n"""
    return sum([x for x in range(2, n) if isprime(x)])

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: denominator-obligation, domain-obligation, in-place-mutation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Add nonzero or positive denominator facts at exact division/modulo sites when expressible.
# Precondition: Add operation-specific domains: sqrt arg >= 0, log arg > 0, acos arg in [-1, 1].
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
import numpy as np

@zk_circuit
def summation(pos: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024'], weights: Float[Array, 'N<=1024'], points: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024']):
    n_points = len(points)
    n_weights = len(weights)
    sum_array3d = np.zeros((n_points, 3))

    @zk_chip
    def compute(i: Integer):
        pxi = points[i, 0]
        pyi = points[i, 1]
        pzi = points[i, 2]
        total = 0.0
        for j in range(n_weights):
            weight_j = weights[j]
            xj = pos[j, 0]
            yj = pos[j, 1]
            zj = pos[j, 2]
            dx = pxi - pos[j, 0]
            dy = pyi - pos[j, 1]
            dz = pzi - pos[j, 2]
            dr = 1.0 / np.sqrt(dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz)
            total += weight_j * dr
            sum_array3d[i, 0] += weight_j * dx
            sum_array3d[i, 1] += weight_j * dy
            sum_array3d[i, 2] += weight_j * dz
        return total
    sum_array = np.array([compute(i) for i in range(n_points)])
    return (sum_array, sum_array3d)

from zinnia import *

# Categories: denominator-obligation
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Add nonzero or positive denominator facts at exact division/modulo sites when expressible.
import numpy as np

@zk_circuit
def tax(d: Float[Array, 'N<=1024']):
    tax_seg1 = d[d > 256303] * 0.45 - 16164.53
    tax_seg2 = d[(d > 54057) & (d <= 256303)] * 0.42 - 8475.44
    seg3 = d[(d > 13769) & (d <= 54057)] - 13769
    seg4 = d[(d > 8820) & (d <= 13769)] - 8820
    prog_seg3 = seg3 * 2.2376e-06 + 0.2397
    prog_seg4 = seg4 * 1.00727e-05 + 0.14
    return (np.sum(tax_seg1) + np.sum(tax_seg2) + np.sum(seg3 * prog_seg3 + 939.57) + np.sum(seg4 * prog_seg4)) / np.sum(d)

import numpy as np

@zk_circuit
def train_eq(E: Complex[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024'], TrSyms: Complex[Array, 'N<=1024'], os: Float, mu: Float, wx: Complex[Array, 'N<=1024'], errfctprs: Float, adapt: Float):
    Ntaps = wx.shape[1]
    pols = wx.shape[0]
    R, symbs = errfctprs
    err = np.zeros(TrSyms, dtype=E.dtype)
    for i in range(TrSyms):
        X = E[:, i * os:i * os + Ntaps]
        Xest = np.sum(np.conj(wx) * X)
        err[i] = (R.real - abs(Xest) ** 2) * Xest
        wx += mu * np.conj(err[i]) * X
        if adapt and i > 0:
            if err[i].real * err[i - 1].real > 0 and err[i].imag * err[i - 1].imag > 0:
                pass
            else:
                mu = np.float32(mu / (1 + mu * (err[i].real * err[i].real + err[i].imag * err[i].imag)))
    return (err, wx, mu)

@zk_circuit
def train_eq2(E: Complex[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024'], TrSyms: Complex[Array, 'N<=1024'], os: Float, mu: Float, wx: Complex[Array, 'N<=1024'], errfctprs: Float, adapt: Float):
    Ntaps = wx.shape[1]
    pols = wx.shape[0]
    R, symbs = errfctprs
    err = np.zeros(TrSyms, dtype=E.dtype)
    for i in range(TrSyms):
        X = E[:, i * os:i * os + Ntaps]
        Xest = np.sum(np.conj(wx) * X)
        err[i] = (R.real - abs(Xest) ** 2) * Xest
        wx += mu * np.conj(err[i]) * X
        if adapt and i > 0:
            if err[i].real * err[i - 1].real > 0 and err[i].imag * err[i - 1].imag > 0:
                pass
            else:
                mu = np.float32(mu / (1 + mu * (err[i].real * err[i].real + err[i].imag * err[i].imag)))
    return (err, wx, mu)

@zk_circuit
def cma_error(Xest: Float, R: Integer, symbs: Float):
    return (R.real - abs(Xest) ** 2) * Xest

@zk_circuit
def mcma_error(Xest: Float, R: Integer, symbs: Complex[Array, 'N<=1024']):
    return symbs.dtype.type((R.real - Xest.real ** 2) * Xest.real + (R.imag - Xest.imag ** 2) * Xest.imag * 1j)
