from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: complex, denominator-obligation, in-place-mutation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: diagnostic/fail-closed candidate
# Precondition: Add nonzero or positive denominator facts at exact division/modulo sites when expressible.
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
# Diagnostic: Complex kernels require Complex dtype support or a fail-closed classification.
import numpy as np

@zk_circuit
@requires(lambda E, TrSyms, Niter, os, mu, wx, modes, adaptive, symbols, method: 0 <= Niter <= 1024)
@requires(lambda E, TrSyms, Niter, os, mu, wx, modes, adaptive, symbols, method: method != 0)
def train_equaliser(E: Complex[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024'], TrSyms: Integer, Niter: Integer, os: Integer, mu: Float, wx: Complex[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024 K<=1024'], modes: Integer[Array, 'N<=1024'], adaptive: Bool, symbols: Complex[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024'], method: str):
    if method == 'cma':
        errorfct = cma_error
    else:
        raise ValueError('Unknown method %s' % method)
    nmodes = E.shape[0]
    ntaps = wx.shape[-1]
    assert symbols.shape[0] == nmodes, 'symbols must be at least size of modes'
    assert wx.shape[0] == nmodes, 'wx needs to have at least as many dimensions as the maximum mode'
    assert E.shape[1] > TrSyms * os + ntaps, 'Field must be longer than the number of training symbols'
    assert modes.max() < nmodes, 'Maximum mode number must not be higher than number of modes'
    err = np.zeros((nmodes, TrSyms * Niter), dtype=E.dtype)
    for mode in modes:
        for it in range(Niter):
            for i in range(TrSyms):
                X = E[:, i * os:i * os + ntaps]
                Xest = apply_filter(X, wx[mode])
                err[mode, it * Niter + i] = errorfct(Xest, symbols[mode], i)
                wx[mode] += mu * np.conj(err[mode, it * Niter + i]) * X
                if adaptive and i > 0:
                    mu = adapt_step(mu, err[mode, it * Niter + i], err[mode, it * Niter + i - 1])
    return (err, wx, mu)

@zk_chip
def cma_error(Xest: Float, s1: Complex[Array, 'N<=1024'], i: Integer):
    d = s1[0].real - abs(Xest) ** 2
    return d * Xest

@zk_chip
def adapt_step(mu: Float, err_p: Float, err: Float):
    if err.real * err_p.real > 0 and err.imag * err_p.imag > 0:
        return mu
    else:
        return mu / (1 + mu * (err.real * err.real + err.imag * err.imag))

@zk_chip
def apply_filter(E: Complex[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024'], wx: Complex[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024']):
    pols = E.shape[0]
    Ntaps = wx.shape[1]
    Xest = E.dtype.type(0)
    for k in range(pols):
        for i in range(Ntaps):
            Xest += E[k, i] * np.conj(wx[k, i])
    return Xest

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: in-place-mutation, sort-permutation
# Expected outcome: diagnostic/fail-closed candidate
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
# Diagnostic: Sort/permutation kernels should remain explicit diagnostic rows until supported.
import numpy as np

@zk_circuit
def tri(score: Integer[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024'], score_t: Integer[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024']):
    score_t[0, 0] = score[0, 0]
    score_t = score[np.argsort(score[:, 1])]
    return score_t

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: denominator-obligation, domain-obligation, in-place-mutation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Add nonzero or positive denominator facts at exact division/modulo sites when expressible.
# Precondition: Add operation-specific domains: sqrt arg >= 0, log arg > 0, acos arg in [-1, 1].
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
import numpy as np
from math import ceil

@zk_chip
def physics(masspoints: Float[Array, 'N<=1024'], dt: Float, plunk: Float, which: Float):
    ppos = masspoints[1]
    cpos = masspoints[0]
    N = cpos.shape[0]
    HOOKE_K = 2100000.0
    DAMPING = 0.0001
    MASS = 0.01
    force = np.zeros((N, 2))
    for i in range(1, N):
        dx, dy = cpos[i] - cpos[i - 1]
        dist = np.sqrt(dx ** 2 + dy ** 2)
        assert dist != 0
        fmag = -HOOKE_K * dist
        cosine = dx / dist
        sine = dy / dist
        fvec = np.array([fmag * cosine, fmag * sine])
        force[i - 1] -= fvec
        force[i] += fvec
    force[0] = force[-1] = (0, 0)
    force[which][1] += plunk
    accel = force / MASS
    npos = (2 - DAMPING) * cpos - (1 - DAMPING) * ppos + accel * dt ** 2
    masspoints[1] = cpos
    masspoints[0] = npos

@zk_circuit
def test(PARTICLE_COUNT: Integer):
    SUBDIVISION = 300
    FRAMERATE = 60
    count = PARTICLE_COUNT
    width, height = (600, 200)
    masspoints = np.empty((2, count, 2), np.float64)
    initpos = np.zeros(count, np.float64)
    for i in range(1, count):
        initpos[i] = initpos[i - 1] + float(width) / count
    masspoints[:, :, 0] = initpos
    masspoints[:, :, 1] = height / 2
    f = 15
    plunk_pos = count // 2
    physics(masspoints, 1.0 / (SUBDIVISION * FRAMERATE), f, plunk_pos)
    return masspoints[0, count // 2]

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: denominator-obligation, domain-obligation, in-place-mutation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Add nonzero or positive denominator facts at exact division/modulo sites when expressible.
# Precondition: Add operation-specific domains: sqrt arg >= 0, log arg > 0, acos arg in [-1, 1].
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
import numpy as np

@zk_circuit
def slow_wdist(A: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024'], B: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024'], W: Float[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024']):
    k, m = A.shape
    _, n = B.shape
    D = np.zeros((m, n))
    for ii in range(m):
        for jj in range(n):
            wdiff = (A[:, ii] - B[:, jj]) / W[:, ii]
            D[ii, jj] = np.sqrt((wdiff ** 2).sum())
    return D

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: denominator-obligation, in-place-mutation, loop-bounded
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Add nonzero or positive denominator facts at exact division/modulo sites when expressible.
# Precondition: Loop trip counts must be finite and tied to scalar bounds or array dimensions.
# Precondition: Use writable fixed/bounded array shapes and record an aliasing policy for mutated inputs.
import numpy as np

@zk_circuit
def weights(input_data: Integer[Array, 'N<=1024 M<=1024'], threshold: Float=0.3):
    n_seq, length = input_data.shape
    weights = np.zeros(n_seq, dtype=np.float32)
    for i in range(n_seq):
        vector = input_data[i, None, :]
        count_matches = np.sum(vector == input_data, axis=1)
        over_threshold = count_matches > threshold * length
        total = np.sum(over_threshold)
        weights[i] = 1 / total
    return weights

from zinnia import *
# Zinnia benchmark contract draft: generated for review.
# Keep kernel body faithful; refine TODO contracts before final benchmark runs.
# Categories: shape-dtype-only
# Expected outcome: compile/prove candidate
# Precondition: Shape/rank/dtype annotations only.

@zk_circuit
@requires(lambda n, m: 0 <= n <= 1024)
@requires(lambda n, m: 0 <= m <= 1024)
def zero(n: Integer, m: Integer):
    return [[0 for row in range(n)] for col in range(m)]
